1	  // drag on mousedown and maximize on double click on Windows and Linux
1	  // while macOS maximization should be on mouseup and if the mouse
1	  // moves after the double click, it should be cancelled (see https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/issues/8306)
1	  const TAURI_DRAG_REGION_ATTR = 'data-tauri-drag-region'
1	  const CLICKABLE_TAGS = new Set([
1	    'A',
1	    'BUTTON',
1	    'INPUT',
1	    'SELECT',
1	    'TEXTAREA',
1	    'LABEL',
1	    'SUMMARY'
1	  const INTERACTIVE_ROLES = new Set([
1	    'button',
1	    'link',
1	    'menuitem',
1	    'tab',
1	    'checkbox',
1	    'radio',
1	    'switch',
1	    'option'
1	  function isClickableElement(el) {
1	      CLICKABLE_TAGS.has(el.tagName)
1	      || (el.hasAttribute('contenteditable')
1	        && el.getAttribute('contenteditable') !== 'false')
1	      || (el.hasAttribute('tabindex') && el.getAttribute('tabindex') !== '-1')
1	      || INTERACTIVE_ROLES.has(el.getAttribute('role'))
1	  // Walk the composed path from target upward.
1	  // Supported values for data-tauri-drag-region:
1	  //   (bare / no value / "true") -> self: only direct clicks on this element trigger drag
1	  //   "deep"                   -> deep: clicks anywhere in the subtree trigger drag
1	  //   "false"                  -> disabled: drag is blocked here (and for ancestors)
1	  // Clickable elements (buttons, links, etc.) normally block dragging,
1	  // but if they themselves carry data-tauri-drag-region they act as drag regions.
1	  function isDragRegion(composedPath) {
1	    for (const el of composedPath) {
1	      if (!(el instanceof HTMLElement)) continue
1	      const attr = el.getAttribute(TAURI_DRAG_REGION_ATTR)
1	      // clickable without explicit drag region 
1	 blocks drag
1	      if (isClickableElement(el) && attr === null) return false
1	      // no attr 
1	 keep walking up
1	      if (attr === null) continue
1	      // explicitly disabled
1	      if (attr === 'false') return false
1	      // subtree drag 
1	 any descendant triggers
1	      if (attr === 'deep') return true
1	      // bare or "true" attr 
1	 only direct clicks on this element
1	      if (attr === '' || attr === 'true') return el === composedPath[0]
1	  // initial mousedown position for macOS
1	  let initialX = 0
1	  let initialY = 0
1	  document.addEventListener('mousedown', (e) => {
1	      // was left mouse button
1	      e.button === 0
1	      // and was normal click to drag or double click to maximize
1	      && (e.detail === 1 || e.detail === 2)
1	      // and is drag region
1	      && isDragRegion(e.composedPath())
1	      // macOS maximization happens on `mouseup`,
1	      // so we save needed state and early return
1	      if (osName === 'macos' && e.detail === 2) {
1	        initialX = e.clientX
1	        initialY = e.clientY
1	        return
1	      // prevents text cursor
1	      e.preventDefault()
1	      // fix #2549: double click on drag region edge causes content to maximize without window sizing change
1	      // https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/issues/2549#issuecomment-1250036908
1	      e.stopImmediatePropagation()
1	      // start dragging if the element has a `tauri-drag-region` data attribute and maximize on double-clicking it
1	      const cmd = e.detail === 2 ? 'internal_toggle_maximize' : 'start_dragging'
1	      window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke('plugin:window|' + cmd)
1	  // on macOS we maximize on mouseup instead, to match the system behavior where maximization can be canceled
1	  // if the mouse moves outside the data-tauri-drag-region
1	  if (osName === 'macos') {
1	    document.addEventListener('mouseup', (e) => {
1	        // was left mouse button
1	        e.button === 0
1	        // and was double click
1	        && e.detail === 2
1	        // and the cursor hasn't moved from initial mousedown
1	        && e.clientX === initialX
1	        && e.clientY === initialY
1	        // and the event path contains a drag region (with no clickable element in between)
1	        && isDragRegion(e.composedPath())
1	        window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke(
1	          'plugin:window|internal_toggle_maximize'

1	  let customProtocolIpcFailed = false
1	  // on Android we never use it because Android does not have support to reading the request body
1	  const canUseCustomProtocol = osName !== 'android'
1	  function sendIpcMessage(message) {
1	    const { cmd, callback, error, payload, options } = message
1	      !customProtocolIpcFailed
1	      && (canUseCustomProtocol || cmd === fetchChannelDataCommand)
1	      const { contentType, data } = processIpcMessage(payload)
1	      const headers = new Headers((options && options.headers) || {})
1	      headers.set('Content-Type', contentType)
1	      headers.set('Tauri-Callback', callback)
1	      headers.set('Tauri-Error', error)
1	      headers.set('Tauri-Invoke-Key', __TAURI_INVOKE_KEY__)
1	      fetch(window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.convertFileSrc(cmd, 'ipc'), {
1	        method: 'POST',
1	        body: data,
1	        headers
1	        .then((response) => {
1	          const callbackId =
1	            response.headers.get('Tauri-Response') === 'ok' ? callback : error
1	          // we need to split here because on Android the content-type gets duplicated
1	          switch ((response.headers.get('content-type') || '').split(',')[0]) {
1	            case 'application/json':
1	              return response.json().then((r) => [callbackId, r])
1	            case 'text/plain':
1	              return response.text().then((r) => [callbackId, r])
1	            default:
1	              return response.arrayBuffer().then((r) => [callbackId, r])
1	        .then(
1	          ([callbackId, data]) => {
1	            window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.runCallback(callbackId, data)
1	          },
1	          (e) => {
1	            console.warn(
1	              'IPC custom protocol failed, Tauri will now use the postMessage interface instead',
1	              e
1	            // failed to use the custom protocol IPC (either the webview blocked a custom protocol or it was a CSP error)
1	            // so we need to fallback to the postMessage interface
1	            customProtocolIpcFailed = true
1	            sendIpcMessage(message)
1	      // otherwise use the postMessage interface
1	      const { data } = processIpcMessage({
1	        cmd,
1	        callback,
1	        error,
1	        options: {
1	          ...options,
1	          customProtocolIpcBlocked: customProtocolIpcFailed
1	        },
1	        payload,
1	        __TAURI_INVOKE_KEY__
